\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 2.

AssertValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
, by
EFrameExpression

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Thus by lemma
1
,
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 3.

AssertIfA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}

By
AssertIfA
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
. Then, since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
,

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
by
EFrameConditionalA
. Also, by
AssertIfA
,
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
. Thus, by induction,

\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 4.

AssertIfB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: Similar to case
D.1
.

Case 5.

AssertAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
, by
EFrameAcc

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, thus by lemma
1

\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.
By
AssertAcc
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
and
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}\subseteq\alpha
. Thus by definition
\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle};\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 6.

AssertConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
, by
EFrameConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{2}
. Also, by
AssertConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
where
\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}\subseteq\alpha
. Therefore by induction
\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 7.

AssertPredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
, by
EFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
and

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
. Also, by
AssertPredicate

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Thus by induction,
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Also, by
EFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
thus by
1

\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
for all
e
.

Therefore by definition
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cup\bigcup\overline{\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}}\subseteq\alpha
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Relating iso- and equi-recursive framing).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\tilde{\phi}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, and
\alpha^{\prime}\subseteq\alpha
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

AssertImprecise
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
:

By
AssertImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi
, thus by
EFrameImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{?}*\phi

Case 2.

AssertValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}e
, by
IFrameValue

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Then by
EFrameValue

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
.

Case 3.

AssertIfA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:

By
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
. Then by
IFrameConditionalA

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\phi_{1}
. Thus by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
and then by
EFrameConditionalA

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
.

Case 4.

AssertIfB
: Similar to case
D.1
.

Case 5.

AssertAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
, by
IFrameAcc

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Thus by
EFrameAcc

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
.

Case 6.

AssertConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
, by
IFrameConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\phi_{2}
. Also, by
AssertConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
where
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha
. Therefore by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{2}
, and thus by
EFrameConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
.

Case 7.

AssertPredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}p(\overline{e})
, by
IFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
.

Also, by
AssertPredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Now, since
p
is a predicate,
\operatorname{predicate}(p)
must be a specification, and thus one of the following cases applies:

Case 7(a).

\operatorname{predicate}(p)
is precise and self-framed: Then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
by definition
6
. Thus by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Then by
EFramePredicate

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
.

Case 7(b).

\operatorname{predicate}(p)
is imprecise: Then
AssertImprecise
must apply in order to derive

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
, and thus
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
, and thus by lemma
8

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Then by
EFramePredicate

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Relating specification assertion and exact footprints).

If
\tilde{\phi}
is a specification and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Proof.

Since
\tilde{\phi}
is a specification, one of the following cases must apply:

Case 1.

\tilde{\phi}
is precise and self-framed: Then by definition
6

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\tilde{\phi}
, and since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, by lemma
3

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
. Therefore, by lemma
2

\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 2.

\tilde{\phi}
is imprecise: Then
\tilde{\phi}=\texttt{?}*\phi
for some
\phi\in\textsc{Formula}
, and
AssertImprecise
must apply in order to derive
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi
, and thus by lemma
2

\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
. Thus by definition
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Relating specification assertion and footprints).

If
\tilde{\phi}
is a specification and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\phi}
is completely precise then
\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

Otherwise,
\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}=\alpha
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Relating specification exact footprints and footprints).

If
\tilde{\phi}
is a specification and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\phi}
is completely precise then
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Otherwise, by lemma
4

\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha=\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Monotonicity of expression framing WRT permissions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
.

Proof.

By
1
,
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
, thus
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, and therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by
1
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Monotonicity of equi-recursive framing WRT permissions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

EFrameExpression
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
: By lemma
7
.

Case 2.

EFrameConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
: By induction

Case 3.

EFramePredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
: By induction and lemma
7
.

Case 4.

EFrameConditionalA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: By induction and lemma
7
.

Case 5.

EFrameConditionalB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: By induction and lemma
7
.

Case 6.

EFrameAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e)
: By lemma
7
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Monotonicity of assertions WRT permissions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

AssertImprecise
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
: By induction and lemma
8
.

Case 2.

AssertValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
: Trivial.

Case 3.

AssertIfA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: By induction.

Case 4.

AssertIfB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: By induction.

Case 5.

AssertAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
: Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
and
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, thus
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
.

Case 6.

AssertConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
:
Then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
where
\alpha_{1}\cap\alpha_{2}=\emptyset
and
\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
by
AssertConjunction
.

Case 7.

AssertPredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
: By induction.

∎

Lemma 0 (Exact footprint preserves equi-recursive framing).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
, then

\langle H,\,\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

EFrameExpression
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
:
By
EFrameExpression

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, thus
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
and
\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by lemma
1
. Then
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
by
EFrameExpression
.

Case 2.

EFrameConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{1}*e_{2}
:
By
EFrameConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{2}
, thus by induction
\langle H,\,\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{2}
.

By definition
\llfloor e_{1}*e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=(\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha)\cup(\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha)
, thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor e_{1}*e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\llfloor e_{1}*e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{2}
by lemma
8
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor e_{1}*e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e_{1}*e_{2}
by
EFrameConjunction
.

Case 3.

EFramePredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
:
By
EFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
, thus by lemma
1

\overline{\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha}
, thus
\overline{\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}}
, and then
\overline{\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
.

By
EFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where

\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
. Thus by induction
\langle H,\,\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
.

Now by definition
\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}\cap\alpha=(\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cap\alpha)\cup\bigcup\overline{(\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha)}
. Therefore by lemma
8

\langle H,\,\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
and

\overline{\langle H,\,\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
.

Thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
by
EFramePredicate
.

Case 4.

EFrameConditionalA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:
By
EFrameConditionalA

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, thus by induction
\langle H,\,\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
, and by lemma
1

\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
, thus
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, and finally
\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
.

Also by
EFrameConditionalA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus by definition
\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=(\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha)\cup(\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha)
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
by lemma
8
and
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by
7
, thus

\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
by
EFrameConditionalA
.

Case 5.

EFrameConditionalB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: Similar to case
D.1
.

Case 6.

EFrameAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
:
By
EFrameAcc

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, thus by lemma
1

\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
, thus
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, and thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. By definition
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by
7
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
EFrameAcc
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Exact footprint preserves assertions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\langle H,\,\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

AssertImprecise
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
:
By
AssertImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
, thus by induction
\langle H,\,\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\phi\rangle\vDash
.

Also by
AssertImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi
, thus by lemma
10

\langle H,\,\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\phi\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}
.

By definition
\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\phi\rangle\vDash
and
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\phi\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\texttt{?}*\phi\rangle\vDash
by
AssertImprecise
.

Case 2.

AssertValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
:
By
AssertValue

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
by
AssertValue
.

Case 3.

AssertIfA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:
By
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
, thus by induction
\langle H,\,\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
.

Also by
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus
\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by
9
.

Thus
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
by
AssertIfA
.

Case 4.

AssertIfB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:
Similar to case
D.1
.

Case 5.

AssertAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
:
By
AssertAcc

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
and
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. By definition
\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\{\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\}
, thus
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
.

Case 6.

AssertConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
:
Let
\alpha_{1}^{\prime}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}^{\prime}=\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha_{2}
. By
AssertConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
, thus by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha_{1}^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2}^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
.

Also by
AssertConjunction

\alpha_{1}\cap\alpha_{2}=\emptyset
and
\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha
, thus
\alpha_{1}^{\prime}\cap\alpha_{2}^{\prime}=\emptyset
and
\alpha_{1}^{\prime}\cup\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
by
AssertConjunction
.

Case 7.

AssertPredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
:
By
AssertPredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
and

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
. Thus by induction

\langle H,\,\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
.

Now by definition
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, thus by lemma
9

\langle H,\,\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
by
AssertPredicate
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Supersets of exact footprints preserve assertions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
and
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By lemma
11

\langle H,\,\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
. Then
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
by lemma
9
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Footprints preserve specification assertions).

If
\tilde{\phi}
is a specification and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\langle H,\,\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By lemma
6
,
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
by lemma
12
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Modeling implies ownership).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
then

\forall\,h\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma):V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha.

Proof.

Let
h
be an arbitrary element of
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
or
\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
. Then one of the following cases applies:

Case 1.

h=\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
for some
f,t,t^{\prime}
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
(and likewise for
\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
), then
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. Therefore
V\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\subseteq\alpha
.

Case 2.

h=\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
for some
p,\overline{t}
:

Then
h\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
since
\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
does not contain predicate chunks. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
,

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
.
\operatorname{predicate}(p)
must be a specification, thus by lemma
4

V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Correspondence with exclusion implies disjointness).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
, then

\forall\,h\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap\alpha^{\prime}=\emptyset.

Proof.

Let
h
be an arbitrary element of
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. Then
V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}
by lemma
14
, thus
V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap\alpha^{\prime}=\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Disjointness implies correspondence with exclusion).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
and
\forall\,h\in\mathsf{H}:V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap\alpha^{\prime}=\emptyset
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.

Proof.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime}).

Let
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
be an arbitrary field chunk in
\mathsf{H}
. Then, by definition,
V\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}
. Thus
\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\cap\alpha^{\prime}=\emptyset
and then
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\notin\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in(\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime})
. Thus

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in(\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}).

Let
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
be an arbitrary predicate in
\mathsf{H}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
.
By definition,
V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}
. Thus
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}\cap\alpha^{\prime}=\emptyset
, and therefore
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}\subseteq(\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime})
. Thus by lemma
12

\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Therefore

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p).

Finally, since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,

\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}^{2}:h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies\llfloor h_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle V,\,H\rangle}\cap\llfloor h_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle V,\,H\rangle}=\emptyset.

Thus all conditions in (
1
) are satisfied, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.

Proof.

Let
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
and
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}.

Let
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Then by lemma
9
,
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Therefore

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p).

Now, since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
,

\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}:h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.

Proof.

Similar to proof of
17
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

Proof.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
,
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
and
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)
.

Also by lemma
17

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and by lemma
18

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Statement rearrangement).

If
s=s_{1};s_{2}
, then
s=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{2}^{\prime}
such that
s_{1}^{\prime}
is not a sequence statement and
s_{2}^{\prime}=s_{2}
or
s_{2}^{\prime}=s_{1}^{\prime\prime};s_{2}
where
s_{1}=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
s=s_{1};s_{2}
. Complete the proof by induction on the syntax forms of
s_{1}
:

Case 1.

s_{1}
is not a sequence statement – Let
s_{1}^{\prime}=s_{1}
and
s_{2}^{\prime}=s_{2}
. Then
s=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{2}^{\prime}
where
s_{1}^{\prime}
is not a sequence statement and
s_{2}=s_{2}^{\prime}
.

Case 2.

s_{1}=s_{11};s_{12}
– Then
s=(s_{11};s_{12});s_{2}
.

Applying the inductive hypothesis on
s_{1}
,
s_{1}=s_{11}^{\prime};s_{12}^{\prime}
such that
s_{11}^{\prime}
is not a sequence statement and one of the following cases apply:

Case 2(a).

s_{12}^{\prime}=s_{12}
– Let
s_{1}^{\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime}
,
s_{1}^{\prime\prime}=s_{12}
, and
s_{2}^{\prime}=s_{12};s_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=s_{1};s_{2}=(s_{11}^{\prime};s_{12}^{\prime});s_{2}=(s_{1}^{\prime};s_{12});s_{2}=s_{1}^{\prime};(s_{12};s_{2})=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle s_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=s_{12};s_{2}=s_{1}^{\prime\prime};s_{2}

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle=s_{11}^{\prime};s_{12}^{\prime}=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{12}=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{1}^{\prime\prime}

which satisfies the original statement.

Case 2(b).

s_{12}^{\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12}
where
s_{11}=s_{11}^{\prime};s_{11}^{\prime\prime}
– Let
s_{1}^{\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime}
,
s_{1}^{\prime\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12}
, and
s_{2}^{\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12};s_{2}
. Then

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=s_{1};s_{2}=(s_{11}^{\prime};s_{12}^{\prime});s_{2}=(s_{11}^{\prime};(s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12}));s_{2}=s_{11}^{\prime};(s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12};s_{2})=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle s_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12};s_{2}=(s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12});s_{2}=s_{1}^{\prime\prime};s_{2}

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle=s_{11}^{\prime};s_{12}^{\prime}=s_{11}^{\prime};(s_{11}^{\prime\prime};s_{12})=s_{1}^{\prime};s_{1}^{\prime\prime}

which satisfies the original statement.

∎

Lemma 0 (Run-time check monotonicity WRT permissions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}r
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}r
.

Proof.

By cases on
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}r
:

Case 1.

CheckValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}t
:
By
CheckValue

V(t)=\texttt{true}
, thus by
CheckValue

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}t
.

Case 2.

CheckAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\langle f,\,t\rangle
:
By
CheckAcc

\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, thus
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}
and therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}\langle t,\,f\rangle
by
CheckAcc
.

Case 3.

CheckPred
–
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
:
By
CheckPred

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
. Then by lemma
9
,
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
by
CheckPred
.

CheckSep
–
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\operatorname{sep}(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2})
:
By
CheckSep

V\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\operatorname{sep}(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2})
by
CheckSep
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Run-time checks monotonicity WRT permissions).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}
.

Proof.

Then by definition
\forall\,r\in\mathcal{R}:\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}r
and then by lemma
21

\forall\,r\in\mathcal{R}:\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}r
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Run-time check set implies subsets).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition
\forall\,r\in\mathcal{R}:\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}r
and
\forall\,r\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime}:r\in\mathcal{R}
, therefore
\forall\,r\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime}:\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}r
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
.
∎

D.2.
Evaluation

Definition 0.

For a judgement
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
, given an initial valuation
V
and heap
H
, the
corresponding valuation
is denoted

V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H].

This valuation is defined as follows, depending on the rule that proves the derivation. Values are referenced using the respective name from the rule definition.

Note that the corresponding valuation always extends the initial valuation, and is defined for all fresh symbolic values in the judgement.

•

SEvalLiteral
:

V[\sigma\vdash l\Downarrow l\dashv\sigma,\,\_\mid H]:=V

•

SEvalVar
:

V[\sigma\vdash x\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma,\,\_\mid H]:=V

•

SEvalNeg
:

V[\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SEvalOrA
:

V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SEvalOrB
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003ptV[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt[\sigma^{\prime}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}]\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEvalAndA
:

V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SEvalAndB
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003ptV[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt[\sigma^{\prime}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{1}]\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEvalOp
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003ptV[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt[\sigma^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEvalField
or
SEvalFieldOptimistic
:

V[\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t_{e}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SEvalFieldImprecise
:

V[\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t_{e}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H][t\mapsto H(V(t_{e}),f)]

•

SEvalFieldFailure
:

V[\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t_{e}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H][t\mapsto H(V(t_{e}),f)]

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
then
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
:

Case 1.

SEvalLiteral
–
\sigma\vdash l\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma,\,\_
;
SEvalVar
–
\sigma\vdash x\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma,\,\_
: Trivial since
g(\sigma)\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 2.

SEvalOrA
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\vee e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
:
By
SEvalOrA

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
, thus by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
. Therefore
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\wedge t_{1}\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 3.

SEvalOrB
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\vee e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
:
By
SEvalOrB

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
and
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
, thus by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
. Therefore
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}\implies g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 4.

SEvalAndA
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 5.

SEvalAndB
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 6.

SEvalOp
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
;
SEvalNeg
–
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
;
SEvalField
,
SEvalFieldOptimistic
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
;
SEvalFieldImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
: By induction.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
then
\iota(\sigma^{\prime})=\iota(\sigma)
,
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})=\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, and
\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})=\gamma(\sigma)
.

Proof.

Trivial by induction on
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

Let
V
be some initial valuation and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle
be some well-formed evaluation state such that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

If
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
where
V^{\prime}\supseteq V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]
, then

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime},\quad\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t),\quad\text{and}~\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
:

Case 1.

SEvalLiteral
–
\sigma\vdash l\Downarrow l\dashv\sigma,\,\emptyset
:
Then
V^{\prime}=V
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
by assumption.

By
EvalLiteral

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash l\Downarrow l
, and by definition
V^{\prime}(l)=l
.

By
FrameLiteral

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}l
.

Case 2.

SEvalVar
–
\sigma\vdash x\Downarrow\gamma(\sigma)(x)\dashv\sigma,\,\emptyset
:
Then
V^{\prime}=V
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
by assumption.

By
EvalVar

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash x\Downarrow\rho(x)
, and
\rho(x)=V^{\prime}(\gamma(\sigma)(x))
since
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)
.

By
FrameVar

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}x
.

Case 3.

SEvalOrA
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
:

By
SEvalOrA

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Then
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
since
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{1}\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
by
SEvalOrA
. Then by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Now
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
since
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{1}]
by
SEvalOrA
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
by assumption.

By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{1})
. But now
V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
since
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{1}\implies t_{1}
. Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
.

Then by
EvalOrA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
by
FrameOrA
since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
.

Case 4.

SEvalOrB
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\vee e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:

By
SEvalOrB

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{2}
, where
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}]
.

Now suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. By lemma
23

\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
(thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
) and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

Also, by lemma
25
,
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
. Therefore
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
by assumption.

Now by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
. Also
V^{\prime}(g(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
since
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
. Then also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{1})
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{2})
. But now
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}\implies\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}
, thus
V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{false}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
.

Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{2})
by
EvalOrB
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
by
FrameOrB
since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
.

Case 5.

SEvalAndA
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 6.

SEvalAndB
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\wedge e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 7.

SEvalOp
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:

By
SEvalOp

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

Now suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. By lemma
23

\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

Also, by lemma
25
,
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
. Therefore
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
by assumption.

Now by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
, and then also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{1})
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{2})
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{1})\oplus V^{\prime}(t_{2})
and
V^{\prime}(t_{1})\oplus V^{\prime}(t_{2})=V^{\prime}(t_{1}\oplus t_{2})
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
by
FrameOp
.

Case 8.

SEvalNeg
–
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
:

By
SEvalNeg

\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
.

Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Now
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
by induction.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
. Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\neg V^{\prime}(t)
by
EvalNeg
and
V^{\prime}(\mathop{\texttt{!}}t)=\neg V^{\prime}(t)
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, and thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
by
FrameNeg
.

Case 9.

SEvalField
:
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}

By
SEvalField

\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t_{e}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
.

Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
by induction.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
. By
SEvalField

g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}^{\prime}
, therefore
V^{\prime}(t_{e})=V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime})
. Also by
SEvalField

\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Therefore
V^{\prime}(t)=H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),f)=H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)
since
\langle H,\,\rho,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)
by
EvalField
and
H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)=V_{\sigma^{\prime}}(t)
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Also, since
\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
and
\langle H,\,\rho,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
,
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),\,f\rangle=\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. By
AssertAcc
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
. Thus by
FrameField

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
.

Case 10.

SEvalFieldOptimistic
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 11.

SEvalFieldImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R};\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
:

By
SEvalFieldImprecise

\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t_{e}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}
,
t=\operatorname{fresh}
, and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}[\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime});\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle]
.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R};\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
by lemma
23
. Then
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Then by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

By lemma
23

\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}
and thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
. Then by
CheckAcc

\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
.

By definition
24

H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)=V^{\prime}(t)
, and also
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime});\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle
.

Then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
since
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
differ only in their
\mathcal{H}
components and
\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime});\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
. As shown before,
H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)=V^{\prime}(t)
. Thus by
EvalField

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Also, as shown before,
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. By
AssertAcc
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
. Thus by
FrameField

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
.

Case 12.

SEvalFieldFailure
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\{\bot\}
:

\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V}\{\bot\}
cannot hold. Since the assumptions cannot be satisfied, the statement holds vacuously.

∎

Lemma 0 (Progress).

If
V
is some initial valuation and
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
, then for some
\sigma^{\prime}
,
t
, and
\mathcal{R}
,

\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}~\text{and}~V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}

where
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\mid H]
for some
H
.

Proof.

By induction on
e
:

Case 1.

l\in\textsc{Literal}
:
By
SEvalLiteral

\sigma\vdash l\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma,\,\_
. Then
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
by assumptions.

Case 2.

x\in\textsc{Var}
:
By
SEvalVar

\sigma\vdash x\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma,\,\_
. Then
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
by assumptions.

Case 3.

e.f
where
e\in\textsc{Expr},f\in\textsc{Field}
:
By induction,
e\vdash t_{e}\Downarrow\sigma\dashv\mathcal{R},\,
and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
where
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation. Then one of the following must apply:

Case 3(a).
If
\exists\,t_{e}^{\prime},t:\big[g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}\big]\wedge\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
, then
SEvalField
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
. Let
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}
.

Case 3(b).
Otherwise,
\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime},t:\big[g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}\big]\wedge\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Then, if
\exists\,t_{e}^{\prime},t:\big[g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}\big]\wedge\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
,
SEvalFieldOptimistic
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
. Let
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}
.

Case 3(c).
Otherwise,
\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime},t:\big[g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}\big]\wedge\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Then if
\iota(\sigma^{\prime})
,
SEvalFieldImprecise
applies. In this case,
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
where
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})
.

Case 3(d).
Otherwise,
\neg\iota(\sigma^{\prime})
and
\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime},t:\big[g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}\big]\wedge\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Thus
SEvalFieldFailure
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
. Let
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}
.

In all of these subcases,
\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
where
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})
, and thus
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. By definition
24
, in all of these subcases
V[\sigma\vdash e.f\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_\mid H]
extends
V^{\prime}
.

Case 4.

e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
;
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
:
By induction
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
for some
e_{1},\sigma^{\prime}
where
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Then by induction
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,
where
V^{\prime\prime}
is the corresponding valuation extending
V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Finally, by
SEvalOp

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
. By definition
24
,
V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_\mid H]
extends
V^{\prime\prime}
.

Case 5.

e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
;
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
:
By induction
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
for some
e_{1},t_{e},\sigma^{\prime}
where
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Then one of the following cases must apply since the program is well-typed:

Case 5(a).

V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
: Then by
SEvalOrA
,
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\vee e_{2}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
where
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{1}
. Let
V^{\prime\prime}
be the corresponding valuation. Now
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))\wedge V^{\prime\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
, which completes the proof.

Case 5(b).

V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{false}
: Let
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t_{1}]
. Then
V^{\prime}(g(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}))=V^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})\wedge\neg V^{\prime}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
. By induction
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow t_{2}\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
for some
e_{2},t_{2},\sigma^{\prime\prime}
where
V^{\prime\prime}
is the corresponding valuation and
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Finally, by
SEvalOrB
,
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_
. By definition
24

V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\_\mid H]
extends
V^{\prime\prime}
.

Case 6.

e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}
;
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
: Similar to case
D.2
.

Case 7.

\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
;
e\in\textsc{Expr}
:
By induction
\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
and
V^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})=\texttt{true}
where
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding derivation. Then by
SEvalNeg
,
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\_\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\_
and the corresponding valuation extends
V^{\prime}
by definition
24
.

∎

D.3.
Deterministic Evaluation

Definition 0.

For a judgement
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
, given an initial valuation
V
and heap
H
, the
corresponding valuation
is denoted

V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H].

This valuation is defined as follows, depending on the rule that proves the derivation.

Note that the corresponding valuation always extends the initial valuation and is defined for all
\operatorname{fresh}
symbolic values in the judgement.

•

SEvalPCLiteral
:

V[\sigma\vdash l\downarrow l\dashv\emptyset\mid H]:=V

•

SEvalPCVar
:

V[\sigma\vdash x\downarrow\gamma(\sigma)(x)\dashv\emptyset\mid H]:=V

•

SEvalPCOr
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=V

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEValPCAnd
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=V

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEvalPCOp
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]:=V

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle[\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{2}\mid H]

•

SEvalPCField
or
SEvalPCFieldOptimistic
:

V[\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SEvalPCFieldImprecise
or
SEvalPCFieldMissing
:

V[\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V^{\prime}[t\mapsto H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)]

where
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]
.

Lemma 0 (Soundness).

Suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

If
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
,
V^{\prime}\supseteq V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
, then

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
:

Case 1.

SEvalPCLiteral
–
\sigma\vdash l\downarrow l\dashv\emptyset
:

Then
V
is the corresponding valuation. By
EvalLiteral

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash l\Downarrow l
and by definition
V(l)=l
.

By
FrameLiteral

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}l
.

Case 2.

SEvalPCVar
–
\sigma\vdash x\downarrow\gamma(\sigma)(x)\dashv\emptyset
:

Then
V
is the corresponding valuation. Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash x\Downarrow\rho(x)
by
EvalLiteral
, and
\rho(x)=V(\gamma(\sigma)(x))
since
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)
.

By
FrameVar
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}x
.

Case 3.

SEvalPCOr
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\vee e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:

By
SEvalPCOr

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{2}
. Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be the respective corresponding valuations, with
V_{2}
extending
V_{1}
and
V_{1}
extending
V
. Then
V_{2}
