is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V_{2}}\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
. Then
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V_{2}}\mathcal{R}_{2}
by lemma
23
.

Then one of the following cases applies, assuming a well-typed program:

Case 3(a).

V_{1}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
:
Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by induction, and then by
EvalOrA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
. Also,
V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})=(V_{1}(t_{1})\mathbin{\texttt{||}}V_{2}(t_{2}))=\texttt{true}\vee V_{2}(t_{2})=\texttt{true}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})
.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
. Since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, by
FrameOrA

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
.

Case 3(b).

V_{1}(t_{1})=\texttt{false}
:
Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{2})
by induction. Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{2})
by
EvalOrB
. Also,
V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})=V_{1}(t_{1})\vee V_{2}(t_{2})=\texttt{false}\vee V_{2}(t_{2})=V_{2}(t_{2})
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})
.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
. Since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
, by
FrameOrB

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
.

Case 4.

SEvalPCAnd
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:
Similar to case
D.3
.

Case 5.

SEvalPCOp
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}

Use the same proof as in case
D.3
up to subcases.

Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow V_{1}(t_{1})
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{2})
by induction. Thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow V_{1}(t_{1})\oplus V_{2}(t_{2})
by
EvalOp
. Also,
V_{1}(t_{1})\oplus V_{2}(t_{2})=V_{2}(t_{1}\oplus t_{2})
by definition, therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow V_{2}(t_{1}\oplus t_{2})
.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
. Therefore by
FrameOp
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
.

Case 6.

SEvalPCNeg
–
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}t\dashv\mathcal{R}
:

By
SEvalPCNeg

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, thus
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case. Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
.

Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
by induction, and then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}V^{\prime}(t)
by
EvalNeg
. Also,
\neg V^{\prime}(t)=V^{\prime}(\mathop{\texttt{!}}t)
by definition. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(\mathop{\texttt{!}}t)
.

Also by induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
by
FrameNeg
.

Case 7.

SEvalPCField
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
:

By
SEvalPCField

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, thus
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case. Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
.

By
SEvalPCField

g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
, thus
V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime})=V^{\prime}(t_{e})
. By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
[
=V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime})
]. Then by
EvalField
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),f)
.

Also by
SEvalPCField

\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. Then
H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),f)=V^{\prime}(t)
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
.

Also,
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. Now
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
. By induction
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
by
FrameField
.

Case 8.

SEvalPCFieldOptimistic
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
:
Same as case
D.3
, replacing
\mathsf{H}
with
\mathcal{H}
.

Case 9.

SEvalPCFieldImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\cup\{\texttt{acc}(t_{e}.f)\}
:

By
SEvalPCFieldImprecise

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case. Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R};\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
.

By lemma
23

\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}
.

By
SEvalPCFieldImprecise

t=\operatorname{fresh}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
, by
CheckAcc

\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
.

By induction
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
, thus
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)
by
EvalField
. But also
V^{\prime}(t)=H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)
by definition, therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
.

Finally,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
and
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
. Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by induction. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
by
FrameField
.

Case 10.

SEvalPCFieldMissing
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\cup\{\bot\}
:

Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case.
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\{\bot\}
cannot hold. Since the conditions cannot be satisfied, the statement vacuously holds.

∎

Lemma 0.

Suppose that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

If
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
and
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
then
v=V^{\prime}(t)
, where
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H]
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
:

Case 1.

SEvalPCLiteral
–
\sigma\vdash l\downarrow l\dashv\emptyset
:

Suppose
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash l\Downarrow v
for some
v
. By
EvalLiteral

v=l
, and by definition
V^{\prime}(l)=l
.

Case 2.

SEvalPCVar
–
\sigma\vdash x\downarrow\gamma(\sigma)(x)\dashv\emptyset
:

Suppose
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash x\Downarrow v
for some
v
. By
EvalVar

v=\rho(x)
. Also
\rho(x)=V^{\prime}(\gamma(\sigma)(x))
since
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)
.

Case 3.

SEvalPCOr
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:

By
SEvalPCOr

\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{2}
. Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be the respective corresponding valuations, with
V_{2}
extending
V_{1}
and
V_{1}
extending
V
. Then
V_{2}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Suppose that
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow v
. Then one of the following rules must be used:

Case 3(a).

EvalOrA
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
thus
v=\texttt{true}
:

Then
v=\texttt{true}
. By
EvalOrA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus by induction
V_{1}(t_{1})=\texttt{true}
. Also
V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})=(V_{1}(t_{1})\vee V_{2}(t_{2}))=\texttt{true}\vee V^{\prime}(t_{2})=\texttt{true}
. Therefore
v=V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})
.

Case 3(b).

EvalOrB
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
thus
v=v_{2}
:

By
EvalOrB

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v
. Thus
V_{1}(t_{1})=\texttt{false}
and by
V_{2}(t_{2})=v_{2}
by induction. Also,
V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})=V_{1}(t_{1})\vee V_{2}(t_{2})=\texttt{false}\vee v_{2}=v_{2}
. Therefore
v=V_{2}(t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2})
.

Case 4.

SEvalPCAnd
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:
Similar to case
D.3
.

Case 5.

SEvalPCOp
–
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{1}\cup\mathcal{R}_{2}
:
Follow the same proof as in case
D.3
, up to subcases.

Then by
EvalOp

v=v_{1}\oplus v_{2}
for some
v_{1},v_{2}
such that
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow v_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
. By induction
V_{1}(t_{1})=v_{1}
and
V_{2}(t_{2})=v_{2}
. Also,
V_{2}(t_{1}\oplus t_{2})=V_{1}(t_{1})\oplus V_{2}(t_{2})=v_{1}\oplus v_{2}=v
.

Case 6.

SEvalPCNeg
–
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}t\dashv\mathcal{R}
:

By
SEvalPCNeg

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, thus
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow v
for some
v
. Then by
EvalNeg

v=\neg v^{\prime}
where
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v^{\prime}
. By induction
v^{\prime}=V^{\prime}(t)
and therefore
v=\neg v^{\prime}=\neg V^{\prime}(t)=V^{\prime}(\mathop{\texttt{!}}t)
.

Case 7.

SEvalPCField
:
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}

By
SEvalPCField

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, thus
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow v
for some
v
. Then by
EvalField

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v_{e}
for some
v_{e}
. Therefore
V^{\prime}(t_{e})=v_{e}
by induction. By
SEvalPCField

g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
and thus
v_{e}=V^{\prime}(t_{e})=V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime})
.

Now by
EvalField

v=H(v_{e},f)
. Also,
\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
by
SEvalPCField
. Therefore
H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),f)=V^{\prime}(t)
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
V^{\prime}
extends
V
. Finally,
V^{\prime}(t)=H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}^{\prime}),f)=H(v_{e},f)=v
.

Case 8.

SEvalPCFieldOptimistic
:
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}

Same as case
D.3
, replacing
\mathsf{H}
with
\mathcal{H}
.

Case 9.

SEvalPCFieldImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R};\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle
:

By
SEvalPCFieldImprecise

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V_{e}
be the corresponding valuation, and let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case, which extends
V_{e}
.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow v
for some
v
. Then by
EvalField

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v_{e}
for some
v_{e}
. Therefore
V_{e}(t_{e})=v_{e}
by induction.

Now by
EvalField

v=H(v_{e},f)
. By definition
29

V^{\prime}(t)=H(V_{e}(t_{e}),f)=H(v_{e},f)=v
.

Case 10.

SEvalPCFieldMissing
–
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\{\bot\}
:
Same as case
D.3
.

∎

Lemma 0.

For any
\sigma,e
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t
.

Proof.

By induction on the syntax forms of
e
:

Case 1.

l\in\textsc{Literal}

Then
l\in\textsc{SExpr}
and by
SEvalPCLiteral
,
\sigma\vdash l\downarrow l\dashv\_
.

Case 2.

x\in\textsc{Var}
:

Since this is a well-formed program, all variables must be assigned before use. Therefore
\gamma(\sigma)(x)
must be defined, and by
SEvalPCVar
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow\gamma(\sigma)(x)\dashv\_
.

Case 3.

e.f
–
e\in\textsc{Expr}
,
f\in\textsc{Field}
:

By induction,
\exists\,t_{e}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\_
. Then one of the following must apply:

Case 3(a).

\exists\,t_{e}^{\prime}:\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\wedge g(\sigma)\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
:
Then
SEvalPCField
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\_
.

Case 3(b).

\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime}:\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\wedge g(\sigma)\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
and
\exists\,t_{e}^{\prime}:\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\wedge g(\sigma)\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
:
Then
SEvalPCFieldOptimistic
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\_
.

Case 3(c).

\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime}:\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\wedge g(\sigma)\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
and
\iota(\sigma)
:
Then
SEvalPCFieldImprecise
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\_
where
t=\operatorname{fresh}
.

Case 3(d).

\nexists\,t_{e}^{\prime}:\langle f,\,t_{e}^{\prime},\,t\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\wedge g(\sigma)\implies t_{e}^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t_{e}
and
\neg\iota(\sigma)
:
Then
SEvalPCFieldMissing
applies and thus
\sigma\vdash e.f\downarrow t\dashv\_
where
t=\operatorname{fresh}
.

Case 4.

e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
–
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
:

By induction,
\exists\,t_{1}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\_
and
\exists\,t_{2}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\_
. Then, by
SEvalPCOp
,
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\oplus t_{2}\dashv\_
.

Case 5.

e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
–
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
:

By induction,
\exists\,t_{1}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\_
and
\exists\,t_{2}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\_
. Then, by
SEvalPCOr
,
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}t_{2}\dashv\_
.

Case 6.

e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}
–
e_{1},e_{2}\in\textsc{Expr}
:

By induction,
\exists\,t_{1}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{1}\downarrow t_{1}\dashv\_
and
\exists\,t_{2}\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e_{2}\downarrow t_{2}\dashv\_
. Then, by
SEvalPCAnd
,
\sigma\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\downarrow t_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t_{2}\dashv\_
.

Case 7.

\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
–
e\in\textsc{Expr}
:

By induction,
\exists\,t\in\textsc{SExpr}:\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
. Then, by
SEvalPCNeg
,
\sigma\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\downarrow\mathop{\texttt{!}}t\dashv\_
.

∎

D.4.
Produce

Definition 0.

For a judgement
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
, given an initial valuation
V
and heap
H
, the
corresponding valuation
is denoted

V[\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma\mid H].

This valuation is defined as follows, depending on the rule that proves the derivation. Values are referenced using the respective name from the rule definition.

Note that the corresponding valuation always extends the initial valuation and is defined for all
\operatorname{fresh}
symbolic values in the judgement.

•

SProduceImprecise
:

V[\sigma\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]:=V[\sigma[\iota=\top]\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]

•

SProduceExpr
:

V[\sigma\vdash e\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H]

•

SProducePredicate
:

V[\sigma\vdash p(\overline{e})\lhd\sigma^{\prime}]:=V[\overline{\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H}]

•

SProduceField
:

V[\sigma\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]:=V^{\prime}[t\mapsto H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)]

where
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H]
.

•

SProduceConjunction
:

V[\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid H]:=V[\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H][\sigma^{\prime}\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}\mid H]

•

SProduceIfA
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H][\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]

•

SProduceIfB
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_\mid H][\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t]\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
, then
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

Case 1.

SProduceImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:
By
SProduceImprecise

\sigma[\iota=\top]\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
, thus by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 2.

SProduceExpr
–
\sigma\vdash e\lhd\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]
:
Trivially
g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 3.

SProducePredicate
–
\sigma\vdash p(\overline{e})\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:
By
SProducePredicate

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\cdots]
, thus
g(\sigma^{\prime})=g(\sigma)
.

Case 4.

SProduceField
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
: Similar to case
D.4
.

Case 5.

SProduceConjunction
–
\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
:
By
SProduceConjunction

\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. By induction
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
and
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
, therefore
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 6.

SProduceIfA
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:
By
SProduceIfA

\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
, thus by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 7.

SProduceIfB
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
: Similar to case
D.4
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
then
\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})=\gamma(\sigma)
.

Proof.

Trivial by induction on
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

Suppose
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

If
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
where
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]
, then

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

Case 1.

SProduceImprecise
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

By
SProduceImprecise

\sigma[\iota=\top]\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, thus
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Then, since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
, by
AssertImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
. Also,
\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
, and furthermore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma[\iota=\top]
.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
by induction.

Case 2.

SProduceExpr
–
\sigma\vdash e\lhd\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]
:

By
SProduceExpr

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, therefore
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[g(\sigma)\wedge t]
. Since
V^{\prime}
extends
V
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
. Then, since
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma
differ only in their
g
components
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
since
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
by assumptions.

Case 3.

SProducePredicate
–
\sigma\vdash p(\overline{e})\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

By
SProducePredicate
, for each
e
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t_{i}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case, thus
V^{\prime}
extends the corresponding valuation corresponding for each
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
.

By
SProducePredicate

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle]
. Since
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
differ only in their
\mathsf{H}
components, proving that (
1
) holds for
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
is sufficient to prove that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

By assumptions,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
thus by
AssertPredicate

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
for some
v
for all
e
. Then by lemma
31

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
for all
e
. Then

\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\supseteq\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}\rangle}=V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H},

where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)

thus
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
by lemma
19
. Now by lemma
15
and since
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,h\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Since
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
is the only addition to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
relative to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Also by
AssertPredicate

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Therefore, since
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
is the only addition to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
relative to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p).

Therefore all requirements for (
1
) are satisfied, and therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Case 4.

SProduceField
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

By
SProduceField

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\_
for some
t_{e}
. Let
V_{e}
be the corresponding valuation, and let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case, thus
V^{\prime}
extends
V_{e}
.

Also by
SProduceField

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle]
where
t=\operatorname{fresh}
. By definition
V^{\prime}(t)=H(V_{e}(t_{e}),f)
.

Since
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
differ only in their
\mathsf{H}
components, proving that (
1
) holds for
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
is sufficient to prove that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

By assumptions
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
, thus by
AssertAcc

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v_{e}
for some
v_{e}
such that
\langle v_{e},\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. By lemma
31

v_{e}=V_{e}(t_{e})
, thus
\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
.

As shown before,
V^{\prime}(t)=H(V^{\prime}(t_{e}),f)
. Therefore, since
\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle
is the only addition to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
relative to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):H(V^{\prime}(t),f)=V^{\prime}(t^{\prime})~\text{and}

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):\langle V^{\prime}(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha

Since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
as shown before,

\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\supseteq\{\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\}=V\llparenthesis(t_{e}.f;t)\rrparenthesis_{H}.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V^{\prime}\llparenthesis(t.f;t^{\prime})\rrparenthesis_{H}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
by lemma
19
. Now by lemma
15
, and since
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,h\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Finally, since
\langle f,\,t_{e},\,t\rangle
is the only addition to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
relative to
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
V^{\prime}
extends
V
,

\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}:h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V^{\prime}\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Therefore all requirements for (
1
) are satisfied and therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Case 5.

SProduceConjunction
–
\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
:

By
SProduceConjunction

\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be the respective corresponding valuations, extending
V
and
V_{1}
, respectively. Then
V_{2}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
, by
AssertConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
where
\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cap\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\emptyset
. Also,
\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Let
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha\setminus\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Then
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by lemma
12
since
\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
. Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
since
\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\alpha\setminus\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Finally, by lemma
34
,
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
, and therefore
V_{2}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V_{1}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Now
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\rule[0.75348pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V_{1}}$}}}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
by induction. Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
by lemma
12
since
\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
. Finally,
V_{2}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
by assumption, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\rule[0.75348pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V_{2}}$}}}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
by induction.

Case 6.

SProduceIfA
–
\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
:

By
SProduceIfA

\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
and
\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. Let
V_{1}
and
V^{\prime}
be the respective corresponding valuations extending
V
and
V_{1}
, respectively. Then
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation in this case.

By lemma
34
,
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t
. By assumptions
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, thus
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t)=V_{1}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t)=\texttt{true}
.

Then also
V_{1}(t)=\texttt{true}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
for some
v
by
AssertIfA
or
AssertIfB
. But by lemma
31

v=V_{1}(t)=\texttt{true}
. Therefore

\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition. Therefore, since
V_{1}
extends
V
,

\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\rule[0.75348pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V_{1}}$}}}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t].

Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by
AssertIfA
since
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
. Finally,
V^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})=\texttt{true}
by assumption.

Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
by induction.

Case 7.

SProduceIfB
: Similar to case
D.4
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Progress).

If
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
, then
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
for some
\sigma^{\prime}
where
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
and
V^{\prime}=V[\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}\mid H]
.

Proof.

Suppose
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
and complete the proof by induction on the syntax forms of
\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

\texttt{?}*\phi
–
\phi\in\textsc{Formula}
:

Let
\hat{\sigma}=\sigma[\iota=\top]
. Then
V(g(\hat{\sigma}))=V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
. Also, since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
, by
AssertImprecise

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
. Thus by induction
\hat{\sigma}\vdash\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
for some
\sigma^{\prime}
where
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Then
\sigma\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
by
SProduceImprecise
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Case 2.

\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
–
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\textsc{Formula}
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
by
AssertConjunction
and lemma
9
.

By induction
\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
, with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
where
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Then by induction
\sigma^{\prime}\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime\prime}
, for some
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
where
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. By
SProduceConjunction
,
\sigma\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, and
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Case 3.

p(\overline{e})
–
p\in\textsc{Predicate},\overline{e\in\textsc{Expr}}
:

For each
e
, by lemma
32
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t\in\textsc{SExpr}
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle]
, then by
SProducePredicate
,
\sigma\vdash p(\overline{e})\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. Finally, letting
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation extending
V
,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
.

Case 4.

e\in\textsc{Expr}
:

By lemma
32
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t\in\textsc{SExpr}
. Let
V_{1}
be the corresponding valuation and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]
. Then by
SProduceExpr

\sigma\vdash e\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation extending
V
, thus
V^{\prime}
extends
V_{1}
.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by
AssertValue
and thus
V^{\prime}(t)=\texttt{true}
by lemma
31
. Finally,
V^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})=V^{\prime}(\sigma)\wedge V^{\prime}(t)=V(\sigma)\wedge\texttt{true}=\texttt{true}
.

Case 5.

\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
–
e\in\textsc{Expr},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\textsc{Formula}
:

By lemma
32
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t\in\textsc{SExpr}
. Let
V_{1}
be the valuation corresponding to this derivation.

Then one of the following rules must apply to produce
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:

Case 5(a).

AssertIfA
:

Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
. Then
V_{1}(t)=\texttt{true}
by lemma
31
and therefore
V_{1}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t)=\texttt{true}
. Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by
AssertIfA
.

Then by induction, for some
\sigma^{\prime}
,
\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]\vdash\phi_{1}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
(extending
V_{1}
) where
V^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})=\texttt{true}
.

Now by
SProduceIfA
,
\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. By definition
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation extending
V
, and as shown before,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Case 5(b).

AssertIfB
:

Then
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{false}
. Then
V_{1}(t)=\texttt{false}
by lemma
31
and therefore
V_{1}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t)=\texttt{true}
. Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
by
AssertIfB
.

Then by induction, for some
\sigma^{\prime}
,
\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}t]\vdash\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
(extending
V_{1}
) where
V^{\prime}(\sigma)=\texttt{true}
.

Now by
SProduceIfB
,
\sigma\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. By definition
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation extending
V
, and as shown before,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
.

Case 6.

\texttt{acc}(e.f)
where
e\in\textsc{Expr},f\in\textsc{Field}

By lemma
32
,
\sigma\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle f,\,t,\,\operatorname{fresh}\rangle]
. Then, by
SProduceField
,
\sigma\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\lhd\sigma^{\prime}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation extending
V
, then
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
.

∎

D.5.
Consume

Definition 0.

For a judgement
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
, given an initial valuation
V
and heap
H
, the
corresponding valuation
is denoted

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta\mid H].

This valuation is defined as follows, depending on the rule that proves the derivation. Values are referenced using the respective name from the rule definition.

Note that the corresponding valuation always extends the initial valuation and is defined for all
\operatorname{fresh}
symbolic values in the judgement.

•

SConsumeImprecision
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\rhd\langle\top,\,g(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\gamma(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset\rangle,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta\mid H]

•

SConsumeValue
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\emptyset\mid H]:=V[\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}]

•

SConsumeValueImprecise
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t],\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R};t,\,\allowbreak\emptyset\mid H]:=V[\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SConsumeValueFailure
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\{\bot\},\,\allowbreak\emptyset\mid H]:=V[\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SConsumePredicate
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash p(\overline{e})\rhd\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}=\emptyset],\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=V[\overline{\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}}\mid H]

•

SConsumePredicateImprecise
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash p(\overline{e})\rhd\sigma[H=\emptyset,\mathcal{H}=\emptyset],\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=V[\overline{\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}}\mid H]

•

SConsumePredicateFailure
:

V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash p(\overline{e})\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\{\bot\},\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=V[\overline{\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}}\mid H]

•

SConsumeAcc
,
SConsumeAccOptimistic
,
SConsumeAccImprecise
,

SConsumeAccFailure
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rhd\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{\prime}],\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\mathcal{R}\mid H]

•

SConsumeConjunction
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}_{1},\,\allowbreak\Theta_{1}\mid H]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.50008pt[\sigma^{\prime},\,\sigma_{E}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})]\vdash\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}_{2},\,\allowbreak\Theta_{2}\mid H]

•

SConsumeConditionalA
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma[g=g^{\prime}],\,\sigma_{E}[g=g^{\prime}]\vdash\phi_{1}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\Theta\mid H]

•

SConsumeConditionalB
:

\displaystyle V[\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]:=

\displaystyle\quad V[\sigma[g=g^{\prime}],\,\sigma_{E}[g=g^{\prime}]\vdash\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\Theta\mid H]

Definition 0.

For a judgement
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\mathcal{R}
, given an initial valuation
V
and heap
H
, the
corresponding valuation
is denoted

V[\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\mathcal{R}\mid H].

The is defined by

V[\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\mathcal{R}\mid H]:=V[\sigma,\,\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\_\mid H]

where
\sigma,\,\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\_
is the judgement used when applying
SConsume
to derive
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\mathcal{R}
.

Lemma 0 (Consume results in more specific path condition (long form)).

If
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
, then
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
:

Case 1.

SConsumeImprecision
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\rhd\langle\top,\,g(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\gamma(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset\rangle,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:
By
SConsumeImprecision

\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}[\iota=\top]\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
. Then
g(\langle\top,\,g(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\gamma(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset\rangle)=g(\sigma^{\prime})
and
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
by induction.

Case 2.

SConsumeValue
,
SConsumeValueFailure
,
SConsumePredicateFailure
,
SConsumeAccFailure
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\_\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
:
Trivially
g(\sigma)\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 3.

SConsumeValueImprecise
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t],\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R};t,\,\allowbreak\emptyset
:

g(\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t])=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 4.

SConsumePredicate
,
SConsumePredicateImprecise
,
SConsumeAcc
,
SConsumeAccOptimistic
,
SConsumeAccImprecise
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\_\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
:
In each respective rule
g(\sigma^{\prime})=g(\sigma)
, therefore
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 5.

SConsumeConjunction
,
SConsumeConjunctionImprecise
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\Theta_{1}\cup\Theta_{2}
:
In each respective rule
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}_{1},\,\allowbreak\Theta_{1}
and
\sigma^{\prime},\,\sigma_{E}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})]\vdash\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak
,  , therefore by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Case 6.

SConsumeConditionalA
,
SConsumeConditionalB
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:
In each respective rule
\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t],\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi^{\prime}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
for some
t,\phi^{\prime}
. Therefore by induction
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t^{\prime}\implies g(\sigma)
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Consume results in more specific path condition (short form)).

If
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_
, then
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By
SConsume

\sigma,\,\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\_
, thus by lemma
40
,
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g(\sigma)
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Soundness of
\operatorname{rem_{f}}
for precise heaps and imprecise states).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\iota(\sigma)
then
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\{(V(t),f)\}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\operatorname{rem_{f}}(\mathsf{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
for any
t
,
f
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}=\operatorname{rem_{f}}(\mathsf{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
and
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha\setminus\{(V(t),f)\}
. Then by definition
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
. In addition,
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
contains no predicate values. Thus by (
1
) it suffices to show that
\forall\,\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,t^{\prime\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}^{\prime}:\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}
.

Let
\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,t^{\prime\prime}\rangle
be some element of
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
. Then by definition
\neg\operatorname{alias}(\sigma,t,f,t^{\prime},f^{\prime})
; then by definition
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee\neg\operatorname{sat}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}[t\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t^{\prime}])
.

Therefore
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(V(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t^{\prime})=\texttt{false})
, and thus
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{false})\vee(V(t)\neq V(t^{\prime}))
. But
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
, thus
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(V(t)\neq V(t^{\prime}))
. Therefore
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\neq\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle
.

Also
\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
. Therefore
\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha\setminus\{(V(t),f)\}=\alpha^{\prime}
.

Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
then
\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})=\gamma(\sigma)
.

Proof.

Trivial by induction on
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\mathcal{R}
then
\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})=\gamma(\sigma)
.

Proof.

By
SConsume

\sigma,\,\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\_
, thus by lemma
43

\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})=\gamma(\sigma)
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Soundness of
\operatorname{rem_{fp}}
for precise heaps).

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\operatorname{rem_{fp}}(\mathsf{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
for any
t
,
f
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}=\operatorname{rem_{fp}}(\mathsf{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
, let
\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}=\operatorname{rem_{f}}(\mathsf{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
, and let
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha\setminus\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}
. By definition of
\operatorname{rem_{fp}}
and
\operatorname{rem_{f}}
,
\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
.

Let
\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,\_\rangle
be an arbitrary field chunk in
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
. Thus
\neg\operatorname{alias}(\sigma,t,f,t^{\prime},f^{\prime})
by definition of
\operatorname{rem_{f}}
. Then
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\neq\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle
:

•

If
\iota(\sigma)
: Then
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee\neg\operatorname{sat}(g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t^{\prime})
, therefore
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(V(g(\sigma))\neq\texttt{true})\vee(V(t)\neq V(t^{\prime}))
. But
V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
, thus
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(V(t)\neq V(t^{\prime}))
. Therefore
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\neq\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle
.

•

Otherwise
\neg\iota(\sigma)
: Then
(f\neq f^{\prime})\vee(g(\sigma)\Longarrownot\Longrightarrow t\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t^{\prime})
. Thus
f\neq f^{\prime}
or
V(t)\neq V(t^{\prime})
. Thus
f\neq f^{\prime}
or
t\neq t^{\prime}
(using syntactic equivalence). But now
\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\cap\{\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f\rangle\}=V\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,\_\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
,
\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle
and
\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,\_\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, and
\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\neq\langle f,\,t^{\prime},\,\_\rangle
. Therefore
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\neq\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle
.

Therefore
\langle V(t^{\prime}),\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}
since
\langle f^{\prime},\,t^{\prime},\,\_\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
.

Also, let
\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle
be an arbitrary predicate chunk in
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
. By definition of
\operatorname{rem_{fp}}
,
\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
; thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t^{\prime})}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p^{\prime})
.

But also
V\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
since
\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle
and
\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\neq\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle
. Therefore
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t^{\prime})}]\rangle}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
by lemma
4
and since

\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\cap\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t^{\prime})}]\rangle}=V\llparenthesis\langle f,\,t,\,\_\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis\langle p^{\prime},\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
. Thus

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t^{\prime})}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
by lemma
12
.

Finally, since
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, the remaining conditions of (
1
) are satisfied. Therefore

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 0 (Soundness of
\operatorname{rem_{f}}
for optimistic heaps).

If
\sigma
is well-formed and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
then
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\operatorname{rem_{f}}(\mathcal{H}(\sigma),\sigma,t,f)
for any
t
,
f
.

Proof.

Case 1.

\iota(\sigma)
: Similar to proof of lemma
42
, replacing
\mathsf{H}
with
\mathcal{H}
.

Case 2.

\neg\iota(\sigma)
: Then
\mathcal{H}(\sigma)=\emptyset
since
\sigma
is well-formed. Then trivially
\langle H,\,\emptyset\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
and thus also
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
by
18
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Soundness of
\Theta
calculation).

If
\phi
is a precise formula,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma_{E}
,
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
where
\alpha^{\prime}\subseteq\alpha_{E}
, then
\langle H,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
and
V\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:

Case 1.

SConsumeImprecision
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\rhd\langle\top,\,g(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\gamma(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset\rangle,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:

\texttt{?}*\phi
is not precise, therefore this rule cannot apply.

Case 2.

SConsumeValue
,
SConsumeValueImprecise
,
SConsumeValueFailure
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\emptyset
:

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by
AssertValue
. Therefore
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
by
AssertValue
.

Also,
V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
.

Case 3.

SConsumePredicate
,
SConsumePredicateImprecise
,
SConsumePredicateFailure
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash p(\overline{e})\rhd\_,\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\}
:

By the respective rule,
\overline{\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_}
for some
\overline{t}
. The corresponding valuation for this case extends the corresponding valuation for all of these derivation.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
,
\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
for some
\overline{v}
by
AssertPredicate
. By assumptions

\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma_{E}
. Thus by lemma
31

\overline{v=V^{\prime}(t)}
, i.e.,
\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)}
.

Let
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
. By
AssertPredicate

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Also,
V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\cap\alpha^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
by lemma
11
.

But
\operatorname{predicate}(p)
must be a specification, thus
V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle}\subseteq V\llparenthesis\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap\alpha^{\prime}
by lemma
4
. Therefore
V\llparenthesis\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
.

Then
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
, and thus
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
by
AssertPredicate
.

Case 4.

SConsumeAcc
,
SConsumeAccOptimistic
,
SConsumeAccImprecise
,
SConsumeAccFailure
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}
:

By the respective rule,
\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t_{e}\dashv\_
for some
t_{e}
. The corresponding valuation for this case extends the corresponding valuation for this derivation.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
for some
v
by
AssertAcc
. Thus by lemma
31

v=V^{\prime}(t_{e})
, i.e.
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{e})
.

By
AssertAcc

\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\in\alpha^{\prime}
. Also,
\{\langle V^{\prime}(t_{e}),\,f\rangle\}=V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H}
, therefore
V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
and also
\langle H,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\{\langle t_{e},\,f\rangle\}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
.

Case 5.

SConsumeConjunction
,
SConsumeConjunctionImprecise
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\Theta_{1}\cup\Theta_{2}
:

By the respective rule,
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\Theta_{1}
and
\sigma^{\prime},\,\sigma_{E}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})]\vdash\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\Theta_{2}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case, thus
V^{\prime}
extends the corresponding valuations for these judgements.

By assumptions,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
and
g(\sigma^{\prime\prime})\implies g(\sigma^{\prime})
by
40
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma_{E}[g=g(\sigma^{\prime})]
.

Then
\langle H,\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
where
\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{1}\cap\alpha_{2}=\emptyset
by
AssertConjunction
, since
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
. Then by induction
\langle H,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
,
\langle H,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
,
V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha_{1}
, and
V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha_{1}
.

Now
V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
and
V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cup V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\cup\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta_{1}\cup\Theta_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
by
AssertConjunction
.

Case 6.

SConsumeConditionalA
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:

By
SConsumeConditionalA

\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\_
for some
t
. Let
V_{1}
be the corresponding valuation.

Also,
\sigma[g=g^{\prime}],\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi_{1}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\_,\,\allowbreak\Theta
where
g^{\prime}=g\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation for this case, which extends the corresponding valuation for this judgement and
V_{1}
.

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
, by
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
for some
v
. By lemma
31

v=V_{1}(t)
. Also, since
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
and
g(\sigma^{\prime})\implies g^{\prime}=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t
by lemma
40
,
V_{1}(t)=\texttt{true}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
.

Also by
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
. Therefore by induction
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
V\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime}
.

Finally,
\langle H,\,V\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
by
AssertIfA
.

Case 7.

SConsumeConditionalB
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:
Similar to case
D.5
.

∎

Lemma 0 (Soundness of consume for precise formulas).

Let
\phi
be some precise formula,
V
be some initial valuation,
H
be some heap,
\rho
be some environment,
\alpha_{E}
and
\alpha
be sets of permissions such that
\alpha\subseteq\alpha_{E}
, and
\sigma
and
\sigma_{E}
be well-formed symbolic states such that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma_{E}
.

Then, if
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E}\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, then

(16)

\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime},\quad\text{and}~\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}\phi.

Furthermore, if the above conditions hold and
\mathcal{R}\cap\textsc{SPerm}=\emptyset
, then

(17)

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi.

Proof.

Suppose that
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
with corresponding valuation
V^{\prime}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E}\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
, and
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Complete the proof by induction on
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\phi\rhd\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta,\,\allowbreak
:

Case 1.

SConsumeImprecision
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash\texttt{?}*\phi\rhd\langle\top,\,g(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\gamma(\sigma^{\prime}),\,\emptyset,\,\emptyset\rangle,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\Theta
:
Since
\texttt{?}*\phi
is imprecise, this rule cannot apply.

Case 2.

SConsumeValue
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R},\,\allowbreak\emptyset
:

By
SConsumeValue

\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the corresponding valuation, with initial valuation
V
. Then
V^{\prime}
is the corresponding valuation for this case.

Thus by lemma
30

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
. Also by
SConsumeValue

g(\sigma)\implies t
. Therefore
V^{\prime}(t)=\texttt{true}
, and therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
. Thus
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
by
AssertValue
.

V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
since
V^{\prime}
extends
V
.

Finally,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by lemma
30
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}e
by
IFrameExpression
, which completes the proof of (
16
).

As shown before,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
by
AssertValue
, which proves (
17
).

Case 3.

SConsumeValueImprecise
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t],\,\allowbreak\mathcal{R};t,\,\allowbreak\emptyset
:

By
SConsumeValueImprecise

\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the valuation corresponding to this derivation, with initial valuation
V
. Then
V^{\prime}
is the valuation corresponding to this case.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[g=g(\sigma)\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}t]
.

By assumptions,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E}\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\{t\}
by lemma
23
. Thus
V^{\prime}(t)=\texttt{true}
by
CheckValue
. Also,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
by lemma
30
; therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
. Thus, by
AssertValue
,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
.

V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
, therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
since
V^{\prime}
extends
V
.

Furthermore, since
V^{\prime}(t)=\texttt{true}
,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=V(g(\sigma))\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}V^{\prime}(t)=\texttt{true}
. Since
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma
differ only in their
g
components,
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\setminus V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Finally,
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by lemma
30
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha_{E},\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmI}}e
by
IFrameExpression
.

Case 4.

SConsumeValueFailure
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash e\rhd\sigma,\,\allowbreak\{\bot\},\,\allowbreak\emptyset
:

\langle H,\,\alpha_{E}\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}\cup\{\bot\}
is a contradiction, thus the lemma vacuously holds.

Case 5.

SConsumePredicate
–
\sigma,\,\sigma_{E}\vdash p(\overline{e})\rhd\sigma^{\prime},\,\allowbreak\bigcup\overline{\mathcal{R}},\,\allowbreak\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\}
:

By
SConsumePredicate
, for each
e
,
\sigma_{E}\vdash e\downarrow t\dashv\mathcal{R}
for some
t
and
\mathcal{R}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be corresponding valuation for this case, thus
V^{\prime}
extends the respective individual corresponding valuations and for each
\mathcal{R}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
by lemma
23

Therefore, for each
e
and corresponding
t
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t)
by lemma
30
. By
SConsumePredicate
, for each
t
,
g(\sigma)\implies t\mathbin{\texttt{==}}t^{\prime}
for some
t^{\prime}
, thus
V^{\prime}(t)=V^{\prime}(t^{\prime})
since
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{i}\Downarrow V^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})
.

By
SConsumePredicate

\langle p,\,\overline{t^{\prime}}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. Since
