D.7.
Preservation

Lemma 0.

Suppose
\Gamma=\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}\rangle
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\langle H,\,\langle\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho,\,s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}\rangle
.

If
\Gamma
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
,
\Pi\vdash\_,\,\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
with valuation
V^{\prime}
, and
\Pi\vdash\Sigma\to\Sigma^{\prime}
for some
\Sigma^{\prime}
such that
\Sigma^{\prime}
corresponds to
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma)
, and
\operatorname{dom}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime}))\supseteq\operatorname{dom}(\gamma(\Sigma))
, then
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a valid state.

Proof.

By definition, it suffices to show that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

Part
21
:

By assumptions,
\Sigma
is reachable and
\Pi\vdash\Sigma\to\Sigma^{\prime}
, thus
\Sigma^{\prime}
is reachable with valuation
V^{\prime}
.

Part
21
:

By assumptions
\Sigma^{\prime}
corresponds to
\Gamma^{\prime}
with
V^{\prime}
.

Part
21
:

Since
\Gamma
validated by
\Sigma
and
V
, the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
. Therefore one of the following cases apply:

Case
20
:

Then
\mathcal{S}=\mathsf{nil}
and trivially the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathsf{nil}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

Case
20
:

Then
\mathcal{S}
is of the form
\langle\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime},\,y\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and, for some
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
,
V^{\prime\prime}
,
x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}
,
t_{1},\cdots,t_{k}
,
\sigma_{0},\cdots,\sigma_{k}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\text{The partial state $\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle$ is validated by $\Sigma^{\prime\prime}$ and $V^{\prime\prime}$},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}),

\displaystyle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k-1}\vdash e_{k}\Downarrow t_{k}\dashv\sigma_{k},\,\_,

\displaystyle\forall\,1\leq i\leq k:V(\gamma(\Sigma)(x_{i}))=V^{\prime\prime}(t_{i}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m)\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})],\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma)=\operatorname{post}(m)

Now immediately from above,

\displaystyle\text{The partial state $\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle$ is validated by $\Sigma^{\prime\prime}$ and $V^{\prime\prime}$},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}),

\displaystyle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k-1}\vdash e_{k}\Downarrow t_{k}\dashv\sigma_{k},\,\_,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m)\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})],\quad\text{and}

Also,
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\Sigma)
,
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime})
, and
\operatorname{dom}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime}))\supseteq\operatorname{dom}(\gamma(\Sigma))
, and thus

\forall\,1\leq i\leq k:V^{\prime}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime})(x_{i}))=\rho(x_{i})=V(\gamma(\Sigma)(x_{i}))=V^{\prime\prime}(t_{i}).

Also, by assumptions
\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma)=\operatorname{post}(m)
.

Therefore the partial state
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime},\,y\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

If case
20
applies:

Then
\mathcal{S}
is of the form
\langle\rho^{\prime},\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\texttt{while}~e~\texttt{invariant}~\tilde{\phi}~\texttt{do}~s\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}
and there exists some
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
,
V^{\prime\prime}
, and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that:

The partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=s(\mathcal{S})

\displaystyle\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma)=\tilde{\phi}

Now
\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma)=\tilde{\phi}
. Then, with the conditions listed above, the partial state

\langle H,\,\langle\rho^{\prime},\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\texttt{while}~e~\texttt{invariant}~\tilde{\phi}~\texttt{do}~s\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

Therefore
\Gamma^{\prime}
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis\langle t,\,f,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\rrparenthesis_{H},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
,
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, and
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle t,\,f,\,t^{\prime}\rangle]
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma);\langle t,\,f,\,t^{\prime}\rangle]
. We want to show
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
.

By assumptions and lemma
19
, it is immediate that
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

Since the
\mathcal{H}
,
\gamma
, and
g
are unchanged in
\sigma^{\prime}
WRT
\sigma
, we have
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
,
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma^{\prime})
, and
V(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
. Thus it suffices to show that
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
.

Let
h=\langle t,\,f,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
, thus
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime});h]
.

We have
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
. Then by lemma
15
,

(18)

\forall\,h^{\prime}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):V\llparenthesis h^{\prime}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Then for arbitrary
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
, if
h_{1}\neq h_{2}
one of the following applies:

•

h_{1}=h
or
h_{2}=h
: WLOG we can assume
h_{1}=h
and thus
h_{2}\neq h
. Then by (
18
),
V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
.

•

h_{1}\neq h
and
h_{1}\neq h
: Then
h_{1}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
,
h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
, thus
V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset
.

Therefore

\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime}):h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset.

Since predicate instances are the same in
\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
,

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle:\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p).

Now let
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
be an arbitrary field instance in
\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Then one of the following applies:

•

\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle=h
: Then by assumptions
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
and
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
.

•

\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\neq h
, and thus
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
: Then
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
and
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
.

Therefore

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})\quad\text{and}

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha.

Finally, since all requirements have been satisfied,
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma^{\prime})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
:

Case 1.

EvalLiteral
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash l\Downarrow l
:
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash l\Downarrow l
by
EvalLiteral
.

Case 2.

EvalVar
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash x\Downarrow\rho(x)
:
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash x\Downarrow\rho(x)
by
EvalVar
.

Case 3.

EvalAndA
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
:

By inversion of
EvalAndA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
. Then
\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition. Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
, and therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
by induction.

Case 4.

EvalAndB
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
:

By inversion of
EvalAndB

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
. Now
\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
by
EvalAndB
.

Case 5.

EvalOrA
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 6.

EvalOrB
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 7.

EvalOp
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow v_{1}\oplus v_{2}
:

By inversion of
EvalOp

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow v_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
. Now
\llfloor e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow v_{1}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{2}\Downarrow v_{2}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\Downarrow e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
by
EvalOp
.

Case 8.

EvalNeg
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash\neg v\Downarrow
:

By inversion of
EvalNeg

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
. Also,
\llfloor\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition, thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\Downarrow\neg v
by
EvalNeg
.

Case 9.

EvalField
–
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H(\ell,f)
:

By inversion of
EvalField

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
. Then
\llfloor e.f\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle};\langle\ell,\,f\rangle
.

Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e.f\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
by induction.

Now
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H^{\prime}(\ell,f)
. Also, since
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e.f\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
,
H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e.f\Downarrow H(\ell,f)
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
:

Case 1.

FrameLiteral
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}l
:

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}l
by
FrameLiteral
.

Case 2.

FrameVar
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}x
:

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}x
by
FrameVar
.

Case 3.

FrameField
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
:

By inversion of
FrameField

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
.

Now since
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
, by inversion of
AssertAcc
,
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
and
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
. Now
\llfloor e.f\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle};\langle\ell,\,f\rangle
by definition.

Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e.f\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
by lemma
59
, and
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
as noted previously. Now
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
.

Also,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by induction. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e.f
by
FrameField
.

Case 4.

FrameOp
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
:

By inversion of
FrameOp

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
.

Also,
\llfloor e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition. Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\oplus e_{2}
by induction.

Case 5.

FrameOrA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
:

By inversion of
FrameOrA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
.

Now
\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by lemma
59
, and
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
by
FrameOrA
.

Case 6.

FrameOrB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
:

By inversion of
FrameOrA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
.

Now
\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow\texttt{false}
by lemma
59
, and
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{2}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{||}}e_{2}
by
FrameOrB
.

Case 7.

FrameAndA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 8.

FrameAndB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e_{1}\mathbin{\texttt{\&\&}}e_{2}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 9.

FrameNeg
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
:

By inversion of
FrameNeg

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
. Also,
\llfloor\mathop{\texttt{!}}e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}\mathop{\texttt{!}}e
by
FrameNeg
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\tilde{\phi}
:

Case 1.

EFrameExpression
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
:

By inversion of
EFrameExpression

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
and then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by assumptions and lemma
60
. Therefore
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}e
by
EFrameExpression
.

Case 2.

EFrameConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
:

By inversion of
EFrameConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{2}
. Also,
\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition.

Now
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}
. Therefore

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{2}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
by
EFrameConjunction
.

Case 3.

EFramePredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
:

By inversion of
EFramePredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e}
,
\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
, where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
.

Now
\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cup\bigcup\overline{\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}}
.

Then, for each
e
and corresponding
x
,
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
by lemma
59
and
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by
60
.

Also,
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
, thus by induction

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\operatorname{predicate}(p)
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}p(\overline{e})
by
EFramePredicate
.

Case 4.

EFrameConditionalA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:

By inversion of
EFrameConditionalA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
.

Now
\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Then
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by lemma
59
and
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by lemma
60
.

Also, Then
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since

\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi_{1}
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
by
EFrameConditionalA
.

Case 5.

EFrameConditionalB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 6.

EFrameAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
:

By inversion of
EFrameAcc

\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
.

Also,
\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
by definition, thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e
by lemma
60
.

Thus
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
EFrameAcc
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
and
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Case 1.

AssertImprecise
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
:

\llfloor\texttt{?}*\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
, thus
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\phi\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
.

Also,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi
by
AssertImprecise
, therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frmE}}\phi
by lemma
61
.

Finally,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
by
AssertImprecise
, therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi
by induction.

Now
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{?}*\phi
by
AssertImprecise
.

Case 2.

AssertValue
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
:

By
AssertValue

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
and
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
by assumptions, therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by lemma
59
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash e
by
AssertValue
.

Case 3.

AssertIfA
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
:

By
AssertIfA

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
, thus
\llfloor\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Now
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
and
\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
thus
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\texttt{true}
by lemma
59
.

Also
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
, and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by
AssertIfA
, therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
by
AssertIfA
.

Case 4.

AssertIfB
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{if}~e~\texttt{then}~\phi_{1}~\texttt{else}~\phi_{2}
: Similar to case
D.7
.

Case 5.

AssertAcc
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
:

By inversion of
AssertAcc

\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
. Thus
\llfloor\texttt{acc}(e.f)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle};\langle\ell,\,f\rangle
.

Now
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
thus
\langle H^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow\ell
by lemma
59
.

Also,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
by inversion of
AssertAcc
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\texttt{acc}(e.f)
by
AssertAcc
.

Case 6.

AssertConjunction
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
:

By inversion of
AssertConjunction

\langle H,\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
for some
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
such that
\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2}\subseteq\alpha
and
\alpha_{1}\cap\alpha_{2}=\emptyset
.

Also,
\llfloor\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\cup\llfloor\phi_{2}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Therefore
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\phi_{1}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
and thus
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{1},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}
by induction. Similarly,
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{2},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{2}
.

Now
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\phi_{1}*\phi_{2}
by
AssertConjunction
.

Case 7.

AssertPredicate
–
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
:

By inversion of
AssertPredicate

\overline{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v}
and
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
.

Also,
\llfloor p(\overline{e})\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle}=\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}\cup\bigcup\overline{\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}}
.

Now for each
e
and corresponding
x
,
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor e\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v
by lemma
59
.

Now
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
. Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto v}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
by induction.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash p(\overline{e})
by
AssertPredicate
.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
for some specification
\tilde{\phi}
and
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

By assumptions and lemma
4

\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
. Therefore
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
since
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha\implies\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
by lemma
62
. Then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
by lemma
9
since
\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
then
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

Proof.

From the assumptions, clearly
\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)
, thus it suffices to show that
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
.

Let
\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
. From assumptions, clearly
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
. Therefore

(19)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})

(20)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha

(21)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)

(22)

\displaystyle\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}^{2}:h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset

Let
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
be an arbitrary field value in
\mathsf{H}
. Then by (
19
)
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
. Also, by (
20
)
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, thus by our initial assumptions
H^{\prime}(V(t),f)=H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
. Therefore

(23)

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:H^{\prime}(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime}).

let
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle
be an arbitrary predicate instance in
\mathsf{H}
. Then by (
21
)
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
. Since
\operatorname{predicate}(p)
is a specification, and
\forall\,\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha:H^{\prime}(\ell,f)=H(\ell,f)
, then

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
by lemma
63
. Therefore

(24)

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}:\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V^{\prime}(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p).

Therefore by (
23
), (
20
), (
24
), and (
22
),
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}
.

Let
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
. From assumptions, clearly
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}
. Therefore

(25)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}:H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})

(26)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}:\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha

Let
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle
be an arbitrary field value in
\mathcal{H}
. Then by (
25
)
H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
. Also, by (
26
)
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, thus by our initial assumptions
H^{\prime}(V(t),f)=H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})
. Therefore

(27)

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}:H^{\prime}(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime}).

Therefore by (
27
) and (
26
),
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}
.
∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}\rangle
is a well-formed state, the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
, and
H^{\prime}=H[\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\mapsto v]
for some
\ell
,
f
, and
v
such that
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
or
\ell
is a fresh value unused in
\mathcal{S}
, then the partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Proof.

For some
n
, let
\mathcal{S}=\langle\alpha_{n},\,\rho_{n},\,s_{n}\rangle\cdot\ldots\cdot\langle\alpha_{1},\,\rho_{1},\,s_{1}\rangle\cdot\mathsf{nil}
.

If
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
:
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}\rangle
is a well-formed state,
\alpha,\alpha_{n},\cdots,\alpha_{1}
are all disjoint. Thus, since
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\in\alpha
, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\notin\alpha_{i}
.

If
\ell
is fresh: Then for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\notin\alpha_{i}
since
\ell
is not referenced in
\mathcal{S}
.

Therefore, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\notin\alpha_{i}
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{0}=\mathsf{nil}
, and for all
1\leq i\leq n
let
\mathcal{S}_{i}=\langle\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i},\,s_{i}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}_{i-1}
.

We prove by induction that, if
0\leq i\leq n
and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i}\rangle
is validated by some
\Sigma
and
V
, the partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
. This is sufficient to prove the main result, since
\mathcal{S}_{n}=\mathcal{S}
and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Suppose
0\leq i\leq n
and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i}\rangle
is validated by some
\Sigma
and
V
.

Case
20
:
Then
\mathcal{S}_{i}=\mathsf{nil}
(and thus
i=0
), and trivially
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathsf{nil}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Case
20
:
Then

\mathcal{S}_{i}=\langle\rho_{i},\,\alpha_{i},\,y\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})\texttt{;}~s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}_{i-1}
for some
y
,
m
,
k
,
e_{1},\cdots,e_{k}
,
s
, and there exists some
\Sigma^{\prime}
,
V^{\prime}
,
x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}
,
t_{1},\cdots,t_{k}
,
\sigma_{0},\cdots,\sigma_{k}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that:

\displaystyle\text{The partial state $\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle$ is validated by $\Sigma^{\prime}$ and $V^{\prime}$},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}_{i})

\displaystyle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k-1}\vdash e_{k}\Downarrow t_{k}\dashv\sigma_{k},\,\_,

\displaystyle\forall\,1\leq i\leq k:V(\gamma(\Sigma_{i})(x_{i}))=V^{\prime}(t_{i}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m)\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})],\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma_{i})=\operatorname{post}(m).

By induction we can assume that if
0\leq i-1\leq n
and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by some
\Sigma
and
V
, the partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Since
\mathcal{S}_{i}\neq\mathsf{nil}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, thus
0\leq i-1\leq n
. Also, in this case the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
. Therefore we can conclude that

The partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

Also, immediately from above,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}_{i})

\displaystyle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}\Downarrow t_{1}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k-1}\vdash e_{k}\Downarrow t_{k}\dashv\sigma_{k},\,\_,

\displaystyle\forall\,1\leq i\leq k:V(\gamma(\Sigma_{i})(x_{i}))=V^{\prime}(t_{i}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m)\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma_{i})=\operatorname{post}(m).

It remains to be shown that
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})]
. But as noted before,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\notin\alpha_{i}
(since
1\leq i\leq n
in this case). Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell^{\prime},\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha_{i}:H^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime},f^{\prime})=H(\ell^{\prime},f^{\prime})
. Therefore by lemma
64
,

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})].

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Case
20
:

Then
\mathcal{S}_{i}=\langle\rho_{i},\,\alpha_{i},\,\texttt{while}~e~\texttt{invariant}~\tilde{\phi}~\texttt{do}~s\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}_{i-1}
for some
e
,
\tilde{\phi}
,
s
, and
s^{\prime}
, and there exists some
\Sigma^{\prime}
,
V^{\prime}
, and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that:

The partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime}$}\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}_{i})

\displaystyle\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime},\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma_{i})=\tilde{\phi}

By induction we can assume that if
0\leq i-1\leq n
and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by some
\Sigma
and
V
, the partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
.

Since
\mathcal{S}_{i}\neq\mathsf{nil}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, thus
0\leq i-1\leq n
. Also, in this case the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
. Therefore we can conclude that

The partial state
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
.

Also, immediately from above,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V^{\prime}$}\quad s(\Sigma^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}_{i})

\displaystyle\sigma\vdash\tilde{\phi}\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma_{i})=\tilde{\phi}

It remains to be shown that
\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}
. But as noted before,
\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\notin\alpha_{i}
(since
1\leq i\leq n
in this case). Thus
\forall\,\langle\ell^{\prime},\,f^{\prime}\rangle\in\alpha_{i}:H^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime},f^{\prime})=H(\ell^{\prime},f^{\prime})
. Therefore by lemma
64
,

\langle H^{\prime},\,\alpha_{i},\,\rho_{i}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}.

Therefore
\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}_{i}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma
and

.

∎

Lemma 0.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
then
V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\phi}
is completely precise, then

V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}=V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset\subseteq\alpha.

Otherwise,

\displaystyle V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=V\llparenthesis\{\langle f,\,t\rangle:\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\}~\cup

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle:\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\}\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle:\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\}~\cup

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\bigcup_{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)}{\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}}

For any
\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
,
\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\alpha
since
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma)
. Therefore

\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle:\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\}\subseteq\alpha.

Also, for any
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
,
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,[\overline{x\mapsto t}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)
where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
; therefore
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
by
4
. Thus

\bigcup_{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)}{\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}}\subseteq\alpha.

Therefore, by the previous calculations,
V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}\subseteq\alpha
.
∎

Lemma 0.

Let
\tilde{\phi}
be some specification,
\hat{\alpha}=V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}
and
\alpha^{\prime}=\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle}
.

If
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
and
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, then
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

Proof.

If
\tilde{\phi}
is completely precise, then

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=V\llparenthesis\emptyset\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=\emptyset

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle}

\displaystyle=\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}.

And thus

\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma\implies\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma.

Otherwise,
\tilde{\phi}
is not completely precise. Now,

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=V\llparenthesis\operatorname{rem}(\sigma,\tilde{\phi})\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=V\llparenthesis\{\langle f,\,t\rangle:\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\}\cup\{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle:\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\}\rrparenthesis_{H}

\displaystyle=\{\langle V(t),\,f\rangle:\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)\cup\mathcal{H}(\sigma)\}~\cup

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\bigcup_{\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)}{\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{t\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}}

Let
\alpha^{*}=\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}
. Since
\langle H,\,\alpha^{*}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma){\rho}
,

(28)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime})

(29)

\displaystyle\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):\langle H,\,\alpha^{*},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)

(30)

\displaystyle\forall\,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathsf{H}:h_{1}\neq h_{2}\implies V\llparenthesis h_{1}\rrparenthesis_{H}\cap V\llparenthesis h_{2}\rrparenthesis_{H}=\emptyset

where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
.

Note that by our calculation of
\hat{\alpha}
,

(31)

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\hat{\alpha}.

Also, by definition
\llfloor\operatorname{predicate}(p)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle}\subseteq\hat{\alpha}
for each
\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma)
when

\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
, thus by (
29
) and lemma
12

(32)

\forall\,\langle p,\,\overline{t}\rangle\in\mathsf{H}(\sigma):\langle H,\,\hat{\alpha},\,[\overline{x\mapsto V(t)}]\rangle\vDash\operatorname{predicate}(p)

where
\overline{x}=\operatorname{predicate\_params}(p)
.

Therefore, by (
28
), (
31
), (
32
), and (
30
),

\langle H,\,\hat{\alpha}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathsf{H}(\sigma).

Since
\langle H,\,\alpha^{*}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma){\rho}
,

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}(\sigma):H(V(t),f)=V(t^{\prime}).

By our calculation of
\hat{\alpha}
,

\forall\,\langle f,\,t,\,t^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}(\sigma):\langle V(t),\,f\rangle\in\hat{\alpha}.

Therefore

\langle H,\,\hat{\alpha}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\mathcal{H}(\sigma).

Also, since
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
,

\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma)\quad\text{and}\quad V(g(\sigma))=\texttt{true}.

Therefore

\langle H,\,\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma.

Now, since
\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle\vDash\tilde{\phi}
, by lemma
5

\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha}
. Thus

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}=\lfloor\tilde{\phi}\rfloor_{\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha},\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha}

\displaystyle\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}\supseteq\alpha\setminus(\alpha\setminus\hat{\alpha})=\alpha\cap\hat{\alpha}

Finally,
\hat{\alpha}\subseteq\alpha\setminus\llfloor\tilde{\phi}\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
by lemma
66
, thus
\alpha\cap\hat{\alpha}=\hat{\alpha}
and then
\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime}\supseteq\hat{\alpha}
.

Therefore by lemma
19

\langle H,\,\alpha\setminus\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma.

∎

Lemma 0.

Let
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle
be some dynamic state validated by
\Sigma
and valuation
V
for some program
\Pi
. If
\Sigma\rightharpoonup\sigma^{\prime}\dashv\mathcal{R},\,\Theta
with
V^{\prime}=V[\Sigma\rightharpoonup\sigma^{\prime}\dashv\mathcal{R},\,\Theta\mid H]
,
V^{\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime}))=\texttt{true}
,
\langle H,\,\alpha(\mathcal{S})\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}
, and
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\to\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle

then
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a valid state.

Note: This is a simplification of theorem
69
, restricted to the particular case of a normal program step (in contrast to a step from
\mathsf{init}
or to
\mathsf{final}
).

Proof.

We proceed by cases on
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}\rangle,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\to\langle H^{\prime},\,\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rangle
.

Case 1.

ExecSeq
:
We have

\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,\texttt{skip}\texttt{;}~s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\to\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle.

Since the initial state is validated by
\Sigma
and
V
,
\Sigma=\langle\sigma,\,\texttt{skip}\texttt{;}~s,\,\tilde{\phi}\rangle
for some
\sigma,\tilde{\phi}
, where
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

Now
\sigma(\Sigma)\vdash\texttt{skip}\texttt{;}~s\to s\dashv\sigma
by
SExecSeq
. Therefore
\Pi\vdash\Sigma\to\Sigma^{\prime}
by
SVerifyStep
where
\Sigma^{\prime}=\langle\sigma,\,s,\,\tilde{\phi}\rangle
.

Now, by lemma
57
, it suffices to show that
\Sigma^{\prime}
corresponds to
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
(with valuation
V
).

Since
\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
, we have
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime})
, and
s(\Sigma^{\prime})=s
by definition. Therefore
\Sigma^{\prime}
corresponds to
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
, which completes the proof.

Case 2.

ExecAssign
:
We have

\displaystyle\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho,\,x=e\texttt{;}~s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle,\,V^{\prime}\llparenthesis\Theta\rrparenthesis_{H}\to\langle H,\,\langle\alpha,\,\rho[x\mapsto v],\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash e\Downarrow v

Since the initial state is validated by
\Sigma
,
\Sigma=\langle\sigma,\,x=e\texttt{;}~s,\,\tilde{\phi}\rangle
for some
\sigma,\tilde{\phi}
where
\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\rule[0.75348pt]{10.44441pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{-7.73688pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\hskip-10.44441pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[10.44441pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma
.

The only guard that applies is
SGuardAssign
, so we have, for some

\displaystyle\langle\sigma,\,x=e\texttt{;}~s,\,\tilde{\phi}\rangle\rightharpoonup\sigma\dashv\mathcal{R},\,\Theta

\displaystyle\text{where }\sigma\vdash e\Downarrow t\dashv\sigma^{\prime},\,\mathcal{R}\quad\text{and (by assumptions) }\langle H,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{V^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}.

where

is the corresponding valuation, extending

.

Let

, then

Case 3.

We have

(35)

\displaystyle\hskip 35.00005pt\langle H,\,\alpha,\,\rho\rangle\vdash_{\mathrm{frm}}e,\quad\text{and}\quad H^{\prime}=H[\langle\ell,\,f\rangle\mapsto v].

, by lemma
19
,

(46)

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\hat{\rho}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\rule[0.75348pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{-10.17577pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\hat{\gamma}].

Now, by lemma
37
, (
45
), and (
46
),

(47)

\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\hat{\gamma}]\vdash\operatorname{post}(m)\lhd\sigma^{\prime\prime},\quad\text{and}\quad V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}

where
V^{\prime\prime}
is the corresponding valuation extending
\hat{V}^{\prime}
.

Let
\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}=\sigma^{\prime\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})[y\mapsto t]]
.

Now (
42
), (
43
), and (
47
), and the definition of
\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}
satisfy the antecedent for
SExecCall
, therefore

\sigma_{0}\vdash y\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})\texttt{;}~s\to s\dashv\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}.

Let
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}=\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime},\,s,\,\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}\rangle
, now by
SVerifyStep

\Pi\vdash\Sigma^{\prime}\to\Sigma^{\prime\prime}.

We want to show that
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\rho^{\prime\prime},\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

Part
21
: Since
\Pi\vdash\Sigma^{\prime}\to\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
,
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is reachable from
\Pi
. Let its corresponding valuation be
V^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Part
21
: By definition,
s(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=s
.

By (
44
)
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})]
. Since the initial state must be well-formed,
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
are disjoint, and as shown before,
\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\rho\rangle}=\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle}\subseteq\alpha
, therefore
\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle}=\alpha^{\prime}
. Also,
\hat{V}^{\prime}\subseteq V^{\prime}
. Thus

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle},\,\rho^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\rule[0.75348pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{-10.17577pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})].

Also, as shown before,
\hat{\rho}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\rule[0.75348pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{-10.17577pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\hat{\gamma}
, therefore

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle},\,\hat{\rho}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\rule[0.75348pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{-10.17577pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\hat{\gamma}].

Then, since
\alpha^{\prime}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime\prime}
,
\alpha^{\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle}\subseteq\alpha^{\prime\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle}
, and thus by lemma
19

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime\prime}\setminus\llfloor\operatorname{post}(m)\rrfloor_{\langle H,\,\hat{\rho}\rangle},\,\hat{\rho}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\rule[0.75348pt]{12.26039pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{-10.17577pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{V}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-12.26039pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[12.26039pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\hat{\gamma}].

Now, since it was shown in (

) that
\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\hat{\rho}\rangle\vDash\operatorname{post}(m)
and in (

) that
V^{\prime\prime}(g(\sigma^{\prime\prime}))=\texttt{true}
, by lemma

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\hat{\rho}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}.

Now by (
44
)
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\rule[0.75348pt]{13.14929pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-13.14929pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[13.14929pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma_{0})
, then since
V^{\prime}\subseteq V^{\prime\prime}
,
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma_{0})
. Now, since
\gamma(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime})=\gamma(\sigma_{0})[y\mapsto t]
, to show
\rho^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\gamma(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime})
it suffices to show that
V^{\prime\prime}(\gamma(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime})(y))=\rho^{\prime\prime}(y)
.

But now
V^{\prime\prime}(\gamma(\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime})(y))=V^{\prime\prime}(t)=\hat{V}^{\prime}(t)=\rho(\texttt{result})=\rho^{\prime\prime}(y)
, which is what we needed to show. Therefore, since
\gamma
is the only component changed between
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}
,

\langle H,\,\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\rho^{\prime\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\rule[0.75348pt]{15.35417pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\hskip-15.35417pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[15.35417pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}.

Therefore, since
\sigma(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we have shown that
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
corresponds to
\langle H,\,\langle\alpha^{\prime\prime},\,\rho^{\prime\prime},\,s\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
with valuation

.

Part
21
: We need to show that the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
. We already have that
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle

,

, or

Then

, thus trivially the partial state

and

.

If
20
applies:

Then
\mathcal{S}^{*}=\langle\alpha_{0},\,\rho_{0},\,y^{\prime}\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m^{\prime}(e_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,e_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime})\texttt{;}~s^{\prime}\rangle\cdot\mathcal{S}^{*}_{0}
for some
k^{\prime},y^{\prime},m^{\prime},e_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,e_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},s^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{*}_{0}
. Also, there exists some
\Sigma_{0}^{\prime},V_{0}^{\prime},x_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,x_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,t_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime},\sigma_{0},\cdots,\sigma_{k^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\text{The partial state $\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{0}^{*}\rangle$ is validated by $\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}$ and $V_{0}^{\prime}$},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V_{0}^{\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma_{0}^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}^{*}),

\displaystyle x_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,x_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}^{\prime}\Downarrow t_{1}^{\prime}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k^{\prime}-1}\vdash e_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\Downarrow t_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\dashv\sigma_{k^{\prime}},\,\_,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k^{\prime}}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m^{\prime})\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha_{0},\,\rho_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\rule[0.75348pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V_{0}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})],

\displaystyle\forall\,1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}:V^{\prime}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime})(x_{i}))=V_{0}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\operatorname{post}(m^{\prime}).

We want to show that the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
and

. Immediately from above,

\displaystyle\text{The partial state $\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}_{0}^{*}\rangle$ is validated by $\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}$ and $V_{0}^{\prime}$},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}\text{ is reachable from $\Pi$ with valuation $V_{0}^{\prime}$},\quad s(\Sigma_{0}^{\prime})=s(\mathcal{S}^{*}),

\displaystyle x_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,x_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{params}(m),

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}=\sigma(\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}),\quad\sigma_{0}\vdash e_{1}^{\prime}\Downarrow t_{1}^{\prime}\dashv\sigma_{1},\,\_,\quad\cdots,\quad\sigma_{k^{\prime}-1}\vdash e_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\Downarrow t_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\dashv\sigma_{k^{\prime}},\,\_,

\displaystyle\sigma_{k^{\prime}}\vdash\operatorname{pre}(m^{\prime})\rhd\sigma^{\prime},~\_,\quad\langle H,\,\alpha_{0},\,\rho_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-1.23135pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\rule[0.75348pt]{14.34724pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{-8.74913pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V_{0}^{\prime}}$}}}\hskip-14.34724pt\raisebox{2.95354pt}{\makebox[14.34724pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}$}}}\sigma^{\prime}[\gamma=\gamma(\sigma_{0})].

Also, the frame
\langle\alpha^{\prime},\,\rho^{\prime},\,y\mathbin{\texttt{=}}m(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})\texttt{;}~s\rangle
must be executing the body of
m^{\prime}
, since it is in the stack immediately above the frame that contains

. Therefore, since

are all parameters of
m^{\prime}
,
y
must be distinct from all of
x_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,x_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}
, since we do not allow assignment to parameters in a well-formed program. Thus

\forall 1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}:V^{\prime\prime}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})(x_{i}))=V^{\prime\prime}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime})(x_{i}))=V^{\prime}(\gamma(\Sigma^{\prime})(x_{i}))=V_{0}^{\prime}(t_{i}).

Finally,

by definition, thus

\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})=\tilde{\phi}(\Sigma^{\prime})=\operatorname{post}(m^{\prime}).

Therefore the partial state
\langle H,\,\mathcal{S}^{*}\rangle
is validated by
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
in this case.
